      "reason": "I independently confirm this specific defect after reviewing the original finding, focused source delta and its argument. Concrete finding: The claim that 'after any finite history for which ... computes a posterior state, the conditional law ... is the posterior supplied' needs a restriction on informative observation availability. The algorithm permits an oracle to return observation-unavailable without restricting that event. Take one candidate, f ~ N(0,1), independent ξ ~ N(0,1), s=0, N=1, and an exact selector. Let the oracle return f+ξ when f>0 and observation-unavailable otherwise. Every call finishes, returned observations have the stipulated additive-noise form, and C₁=2 is positive definite. A successful terminal history reveals f>0, whereas the computed N(y/2,1/2) posterior assigns positive probability to f<0. The final sentence about unavailable observations does not address this information in successful histories. The true-posterior interpretation in the algorithm has the same missing qualification.",

Curtis, and Jorge Nocedal, Optimization Methods for Large-Scale Machine Learning, SIAM Review 60(2) (2018)\"\n      url: https://leon.bottou.org/publications/pdf/tr-optml-2016.pdf\n      locator: Section 5.3.1, Algorithm 5.1 and its gradient-work discussion.\n---\n\n## Definition\n\nLet n,d>=1 and let f_1,...,f_n:R^d->R be continuously differentiable convex functions. Define the finite-sum objective\n\n$$\nF(x)=\\frac1n\\sum_{i=1}^n f_i(x),\\qquad \\nabla F(x)=\\frac1n\\sum_{i=1}^n\\nabla f_i(x),\n$$\n\nwhere the gradient identity follows from [[lem-finite-sum-gradient-is-average]] and the sums use [[def-finite-sum]]. Suppose each component gradient is L-Lipschitz for a common L>0. An SVRG epoch with snapshot \\tilde x and inner length m>=1 is:\n\n1. Compute the full gradient \\nabla F(\\tilde x), set x_0=\\tilde x.\n2. For t=0,...,m-1, draw I_t uniformly from {1,...,n}, independently of the past, and set\n   $$\n   v_t=\\nabla f_{I_t}(x_t)-\\nabla f_{I_t}(\\tilde x)+\\nabla F(\\tilde x),\\qquad x_{t+1}=x_t-\\eta v_t\n   $$\n   for a selected step size \\eta>0.\n3. Independently draw J uniformly from {0,...,m-1} and return x_J as the next snapshot.\n\nThe displayed procedure evaluates the snapshot component gradient at every inner update rather than caching those values. Its correction has the control-variate form of [[def-control-variate-estimator]] with coefficient one and the known finite-sum reference mean; the conditional properties needed here are proved in local results on this page. Conditional-expectation notation inherits the [[def-axiom-of-choice]] assumption from [[def-stochastic-gradient-oracle-and-unconstrained-update]].\n171:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n406:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n618:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n797:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n1029:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n1191:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n1486:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n1728:          \"case\": \"degenerate\",\n",

      "native_log_record": "exec\n/bin/bash -lc 'cat .autopilot/step5-shared-write.lock/owner.json\ncat items/thm-dimer-mode-perturbation-and-local-saddle-error.md items/thm-local-index-one-saddle-certificate-from-enclosures.md' in /home/lazyinspirit/Projects/prestige-math-library\n succeeded in 0ms:\n{\"owner\":\"step5-v2-initial-r1-u51\",\"at\":\"2026-10-17T02:31:18.807Z\"}\n---\nid: thm-dimer-mode-perturbation-and-local-saddle-error\nkind: theorem\ntitle: \"A separated dimer mode gives a local inexact saddle error bound\"\nstatus: draft\norigin: pipeline\npipeline_run: numerical-optimization-dynamics-2026-10-16\ndependency_level: 3\ndeps:\n  - alg-finite-budget-dimer-mode-following\n  - def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian\n  - lem-dimer-central-force-hessian-vector-error\n  - thm-local-index-one-saddle-certificate-from-enclosures\n  - def-ck-and-multi-index-notation-in-several-variables\n  - cor-hessian-is-symmetric\n  - cor-real-spectral-theorem-for-self-adjoint-endomorphisms\n  - thm-cauchy-schwarz-and-the-euclidean-norm\nproof_strategy: direct\nprovenance:\n  statement: ai-altered\n  proof: ai-generated\nverification:\n  precheck: pending\nsources:\n  references:\n    - title: \"Graeme Henkelman and Hannes Jónsson, A dimer method for finding saddle points on high dimensional potential surfaces using only first derivatives\"\n      url: \"https://hj.hi.is/papers/paperDimer.pdf\"\n      locator: \"§II A–C, equations (2)–(3), (18), and (21): paired-force curvature estimate, rotation, and saddle translation\"\n    - title: \"Tobin A. Driscoll and Richard J. Braun, Fundamentals of Numerical Computation, version 1.0\"\n      url: \"https://fncbook.github.io/v1.0/nonlineqn/newtonsys.html\"\n      locator: \"Chapter 4.5.2, equations (90)–(93): the local root correction is a vector Newton solve; the quantitative certificate here is proved independently\"\n---\n\n## Statement\n\nLet $d\\ge2$, let $U\\subseteq\\mathbb R^d$ be open, and let $V\\in C^4(U,\\mathbb R)$ using the convention of [[def-ck-and-multi-index-notation-in-several-variables]]. Fix $x\\in U$. Put $H=\\nabla^2V(x)$ and suppose a validated ordered eigenvalue enclosure certifies index one with gap $\\gamma>0$ as in [[def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian]], so $\\lambda_1(H)\\le-\\gamma$ and $\\lambda_j(H)\\ge\\gamma$ for $j\\ge2$. Let $\\epsilon>0$, let $v$ be a unit direction accepted by the rotation test of [[alg-finite-budget-dimer-mode-following]], and assume the whole segment $x+[-\\epsilon,\\epsilon]v$ lies in $U$. Let $D_\\epsilon$ be the paired-force estimate, with $\\|(I-vv^T)D_\\epsilon\\|_2\\le\\tau_{\\rm rot}$. Suppose each force evaluation has Euclidean error at most $\\eta$ and the third derivatives of each gradient coordinate along this segment are bounded by $M_4$. Set\n\n$$\\delta:=\\frac{\\sqrt d\\,M_4}{6}\\epsilon^2+\\frac{\\eta}{\\epsilon}.$$\n\nIf $\\delta\\le\\gamma/4$ and the measured Rayleigh estimate $v^TD_\\epsilon\\le-\\gamma/2$, then the sine of the angle from $v$ to the lowest-eigenvalue line of $H$ satisfies\n\n$$\\sin\\angle(v,q_1):=\\sqrt{1-|q_1^Tv|^2}\\le\\frac{4(\\tau_{\\rm rot}+\\delta)}{5\\gamma},$$\n\nwhere $q_1$ is any unit eigenvector for $\\lambda_1(H)$. This is a conditional mode bound; rotation success alone is not an index certificate.\n\nLet $\\epsilon_g=\\|\\nabla V(x)\\|_2$. If $\\epsilon_g=0$, $x$ itself is an index-one critical point. If $\\epsilon_g>0$, put $\\rho=2\\epsilon_g/\\gamma$ and additionally assume $B_2(x,2\\rho)\\subset U$, $H(\\cdot)=\\nabla^2V(\\cdot)$ is $L$-Lipschitz on that ball, and $L\\rho/\\gamma\\le1/2$. Then there is a unique index-one critical point $x_*$ in $\\overline B_2(x,\\rho)$ and\n\n$$\\|x_*-x\\|_2\\le\\rho=\\frac{2\\epsilon_g}{\\gamma}.$$\n\nThe dimer direction bound does not replace the validated eigenvalue enclosure or the residual, inverse, and Hessian-Lipschitz conditions in this local saddle certificate.\n\n## Facts & Assumptions\n\n**Given:** The potential and point, validated ordered eigenvalue enclosure, accepted unit dimer direction, paired-force estimate and error data, gradient residual, and (when $\\epsilon_g>0$) the local Hessian-Lipschitz hypotheses in the Statement.\n\n[A1] The validated intervals establish $\\lambda_1(H)\\le-\\gamma$ and $\\lambda_j(H)\\ge\\gamma$ for $j\\ge2$ ([[def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian]]).\n\n[A2] The dimer rotation test has accepted $v$ with $\\|(I-vv^T)D_\\epsilon\\|_2\\le\\tau_{\\rm rot}$, and $v^TD_\\epsilon\\le-\\gamma/2$.\n\n[A3] The force errors and third-derivative bound give the stated value $\\delta$.\n\n[A4] For the local saddle conclusion with $\\epsilon_g>0$, the stated ball, Hessian-Lipschitz, and radius hypotheses hold.\n\n[F1] Since $V\\in C^4$, it is $C^2$; [[cor-hessian-is-symmetric]] makes $H$ symmetric, hence self-adjoint in the standard Euclidean inner product, so [[cor-real-spectral-theorem-for-self-adjoint-endomorphisms]] gives an orthonormal eigenbasis.\n\n[F2] [[lem-dimer-central-force-hessian-vector-error]]: $\\|D_\\epsilon-Hv\\|_2\\le\\delta$ under the stated paired-force and derivative-error bounds.\n\n[F3] [[thm-cauchy-schwarz-and-the-euclidean-norm]]: for a unit vector $v$, $|v^Tw|\\le\\|w\\|_2$; orthogonal projection $I-vv^T$ does not increase Euclidean norm, by the orthogonal decomposition into parallel and perpendicular parts.\n\n[F4] [[thm-local-index-one-saddle-certificate-from-enclosures]]: the eigenvalue gap, gradient residual, inverse bound, and local Hessian-Lipschitz/radius conditions give the stated unique nearby index-one critical point.\n\n\n\n## Proof\n\n**Proof technique:** direct.\n\n1.1 By [F1], choose an orthonormal eigenbasis $q_1,\\ldots,q_d$ for $H$, ordered so that their eigenvalues are $\\lambda_1\\le\\cdots\\le\\lambda_d$. Since $v$ is unit, write $v=\\sum_{j=1}^d c_jq_j$ with $\\sum_jc_j^2=1$. [A1, F1, given]\n\n2.1 Put $\\mu=v^THv$. From [F2] and [F3], $|v^T(D_\\epsilon-Hv)|\\le\\delta$, so $\\mu\\le v^TD_\\epsilon+\\delta\\le-\\gamma/2+\\gamma/4=-\\gamma/4$. [A2, A3, F2, F3, step 1.1, algebra]\n\n3.1 Since $v^THv=\\mu$, $(I-vv^T)Hv=(H-\\mu I)v$. By [A2], [F2], and the projection bound in [F3], $\\|(H-\\mu I)v\\|_2\\le\\|(I-vv^T)D_\\epsilon\\|_2+\\|(I-vv^T)(Hv-D_\\epsilon)\\|_2\\le\\tau_{\\rm rot}+\\delta$. [A2, A3, F2, F3, step 2.1, algebra]\n\n4.1 For every $j\\ge2$, [A1] and step 2.1 give $\\lambda_j-\\mu\\ge\\gamma+\\gamma/4=5\\gamma/4$. Expanding step 3.1 in the eigenbasis from step 1.1 yields $\\left(\\frac{5\\gamma}{4}\\right)^2\\sum_{j=2}^dc_j^2\\le\\sum_{j=2}^d(\\lambda_j-\\mu)^2c_j^2\\le\\|(H-\\mu I)v\\|_2^2\\le(\\tau_{\\rm rot}+\\delta)^2$. As $\\sum_{j=2}^dc_j^2=1-|q_1^Tv|^2$, taking nonnegative square roots proves the stated sine-angle bound. [A1, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 3.1, algebra]\n\n5.1 If $\\epsilon_g=0$, the zero-residual branch of [[thm-local-index-one-saddle-certificate-from-enclosures]] and [A1] make $x$ an exact index-one critical point. If $\\epsilon_g>0$, [A1] and [A4] are exactly the enclosure, residual, and neighborhood hypotheses of [F4]; applying it gives the unique index-one critical point in the radius-$\\rho$ ball and the bound $\\|x_*-x\\|_2\\le2\\epsilon_g/\\gamma$. This local error conclusion is independent of the mode-angle estimate and adds no global convergence claim for the finite dimer iteration. [A1, A4, F4, step 4.1, given] ∎\n\n---\nid: thm-local-index-one-saddle-certificate-from-enclosures\nkind: theorem\ntitle: \"Validated Hessian enclosures and a small gradient residual certify a nearby index-one saddle\"\nstatus: draft\norigin: pipeline\npipeline_run: numerical-optimization-dynamics-2026-10-16\ndependency_level: 2\ndeps:\n  - def-regular-minimum-energy-path-in-a-collision-free-tube\n  - def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian\n  - lem-local-root-from-uniformly-small-residual\n  - thm-courant-fischer-min-max-principle\n  - cor-hessian-is-symmetric\n  - cor-real-spectral-theorem-for-self-adjoint-endomorphisms\n  - def-hessian-and-euclidean-critical-point\n  - def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient\n  - thm-total-derivative-computes-directional-and-partial-derivatives\n  - thm-operator-norm-is-the-largest-singular-value\n  - thm-cauchy-schwarz-and-the-euclidean-norm\nproof_strategy: direct\nprovenance:\n  statement: ai-altered\n  proof: ai-generated\nverification:\n  precheck: pending\nsources:\n  references:\n    - title: \"Graeme Henkelman, Blas P. Uberuaga, and Hannes Jónsson, A climbing image nudged elastic band method for finding saddle points and minimum energy paths\"\n      url: \"https://www.math.pku.edu.cn/teachers/litj/notes/stoch_topics2017/lect04/climbimageNEB.pdf\"\n      locator: \"§I: a first-order saddle has one negative-curvature mode; interval validation and residual transfer are proved here\"\n    - title: \"Xuanyu Liu, Huajie Chen, and Christoph Ortner, Convergence of the Discrete Minimum Energy Path\"\n      url: \"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2204.07467\"\n      locator: \"§2.1: C4 potential and index-one saddle hypotheses used in rigorous MEP analysis\"\n---\n\n## Statement\n\nLet $d\\ge2$, let $V\\in C^4(U,\\mathbb R)$ on an open $U\\subseteq\\mathbb R^d$, and let $x\\in U$. Put $H=\\nabla^2V(x)$ and $\\epsilon_g=\\|\\nabla V(x)\\|_2$. Suppose a validated ordered eigenvalue enclosure certifies index one with gap $\\gamma>0$ in the sense of [[def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian]]. If $\\epsilon_g=0$, then $x$ is an exact critical point with Hessian index one.\n\nIf $\\epsilon_g>0$, put $\\rho=2\\epsilon_g/\\gamma$. Assume $B_2(x,2\\rho)\\subset U$, the Hessian is $L$-Lipschitz in the induced Euclidean operator norm there, and $L\\rho/\\gamma\\le1/2$. Then there is exactly one critical point $x_*$ in $\\overline B_2(x,\\rho)$, it satisfies $\\|x_*-x\\|_2\\le\\rho$, and its Hessian has exactly one negative eigenvalue and no zero eigenvalues. The reported residual $\\epsilon_g$, gap $\\gamma$, conditioning bound $1/\\gamma$, Hessian Lipschitz constant $L$, and radius condition are part of the certificate; a residual or a Ritz value alone is not a certificate.\n\n## Facts & Assumptions\n\n**Given:** The potential, point, gradient residual, interval evidence, gap, and local Hessian-Lipschitz hypotheses in the Statement.\n\n[F1] [[def-validated-eigenvalue-enclosures-for-an-index-one-hessian]]: the validated intervals certify $\\lambda_1(H)\\le-\\gamma$ and $\\lambda_j(H)\\ge\\gamma$ for $j\\ge2$.\n\n[F2] Since $V\\in C^4$, it is $C^2$, and [[cor-hessian-is-symmetric]] gives $H^T=H$. In the standard Euclidean inner product this is a self-adjoint endomorphism, so [[cor-real-spectral-theorem-for-self-adjoint-endomorphisms]] gives an orthonormal eigenbasis.\n\n[F3] [[def-hessian-and-euclidean-critical-point]] and [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]] define $H$ and the gradient. [[thm-total-derivative-computes-directional-and-partial-derivatives]] identifies the matrix of $D(\\nabla V)$ with the gradient Jacobian; [[cor-hessian-is-symmetric]] makes its entries agree with the Hessian convention. A critical point is a zero of the gradient.\n\n[F4] [[lem-local-root-from-uniformly-small-residual]]: if $DF(x)$ is invertible, has inverse norm at most $\\beta$, and is $L$-Lipschitz on a sufficiently large ball, then $\\|F(x)\\|$ small enough gives a unique nearby zero within $2\\beta\\|F(x)\\|$.\n\n[F5] [[thm-courant-fischer-min-max-principle]]: the largest eigenvalue is the maximum Rayleigh quotient, and the second largest is at most the maximum Rayleigh quotient on the orthogonal complement of a leading eigenvector.\n\n[F6] [[thm-operator-norm-is-the-largest-singular-value]]: the maximum defining the finite-dimensional Euclidean operator norm exists, and its definition bounds $\\|Bw\\|_2$ by $\\|B\\|_{\\rm op}\\|w\\|_2$.\n\n[F7] [[thm-cauchy-schwarz-and-the-euclidean-norm]]: $|u^Tw|\\le\\|u\\|_2\\|w\\|_2$.\n\n\n\n**Given:** The data in the Statement.\n\n## Proof\n\n1.1 By [F1], the eigenvalues of $H$ are all at least distance $\\gamma$ from zero. Using the orthonormal eigenbasis in [F2], $H$ is invertible and each unit vector is expanded by $H^{-1}$ by at most $1/\\gamma$; [F6] therefore gives $\\|H^{-1}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le1/\\gamma$. [F1, F2, F6, given, algebra]\n\n2.1 If $\\epsilon_g=0$, [F3] says $x$ is critical, and [F1] says its Hessian has exactly one negative eigenvalue. Suppose henceforth $\\epsilon_g>0$ and use the inverse bound $\\beta=1/\\gamma$ from step 1.1. The radius in [F4] is then exactly $2\\beta\\epsilon_g=\\rho$, and its Lipschitz condition is $\\beta L\\rho=L\\rho/\\gamma\\le1/2$. Since $B_2(x,2\\rho)\\subset U$, every hypothesis of [F4] holds on the stated ball, so there is exactly one zero $x_*$ of $\\nabla V$ in $\\overline B_2(x,\\rho)$ and $\\|x_*-x\\|_2\\le\\rho$. [F1, F3, F4, given, step 1.1, algebra]\n\n3.1 Set $A=-H(x)$ and $E=-(H(x_*)-H(x))$. Then $A$ is symmetric with largest eigenvalue at least $\\gamma$ and second-largest at most $-\\gamma$. The Lipschitz hypothesis is in the induced Euclidean operator norm, so $\\|E\\|_{\\rm op}\\le L\\rho\\le\\gamma/2$ by step 2.1. [F1, F2, step 2.1, given, algebra]\n\n4.1 Let $q_1$ be a unit leading eigenvector of $A$. The maximum Rayleigh formula in [F5] yields $\\lambda_1(A+E)\\ge q_1^T(A+E)q_1\\ge\\lambda_1(A)-\\|E\\|_{\\rm op}\\ge\\gamma/2>0$ by [F6, F7]. The second-eigenvalue min-max formula in [F5], applied to $q_1^\\perp$, yields $\\lambda_2(A+E)\\le\\lambda_2(A)+\\|E\\|_{\\rm op}\\le-\\gamma/2<0$. Every later eigenvalue is at most the second, so $A+E=-H(x_*)$ has exactly one positive eigenvalue and no zero eigenvalue. Thus $H(x_*)$ has exactly one negative eigenvalue and no zero eigenvalues.     "affected_use": "Let $K\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact and convex, let $f\\in C^2(U,\\mathbb R^d)$, and suppose on $K$ that $\\|f\\|\\le F$, $\\|Df\\|\\le L$, and $\\|D^2f\\|\\le H$. For $0<h\\le h_0$, assume $M_h(y):=I-hDf(y)$ is invertible for every $y\\in K$ with $\\|M_h(y)^{-1}\\|\\le B$. The exact-arithmetic autonomous exact-Jacobian Rosenbrock-Euler map $\\Phi_h(y)=y+hM_h(y)^{-1}f(y)$ of [[def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map]], with $\\gamma=1$, then satisfies\n$$\n\\|\\Phi_h(u)-\\Phi_h(v)\\|\\le(1+L_*h)\\|u-v\\|,\\qquad L_*:=BL+h_0B^2HF,\n$$\nfor all $u,v\\in K$.\n\n[F1] [[def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map]]: in the autonomous exact-Jacobian branch with $\\gamma=1$, the exact-method map is $\\Phi_h(y)=y+hM_h(y)^{-1}f(y)$ when $M_h(y)$ is invertible.",

  {
    "ledger": "research/published-consumer-supplier-ledger.md",
    "supplier": "def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map",
    "consumer": "thm-rosenbrock-euler-a-l-stable",
    "status": "examined-unaffected",
    "path": "items/thm-rosenbrock-euler-a-l-stable.md",
    "affected_use": "Consider the exact-arithmetic Rosenbrock-Euler map of [[def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map]] with $\\gamma=1$, exact Jacobian, and its complex scalar test equation $y'=\\lambda y$, $z=h\\lambda$. The complexification is the real two-dimensional exact map under $(\\operatorname{Re}y,\\operatorname{Im}y)$, with multiplication by $\\lambda$ represented by $C_\\lambda=\\begin{pmatrix}\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda&-\\operatorname{Im}\\lambda\\\\\\operatorname{Im}\\lambda&\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda\\end{pmatrix}$. Thus its exact stage equation is $(I_2-hC_\\lambda)k=C_\\lambda y$, equivalently $(1-z)k=\\lambda y$, and its update is $y+hk$. This is the $\\gamma=1$ exact complexification used in [[thm-rosenbrock-euler-scalar-a-and-l-stability]], not execution of a real rounded solver. Its amplification factor is $R(z)=(1-z)^{-1}$. A-stability means no pole and $|R(z)|\\le1$ throughout $\\operatorname{Re}z\\le0$; L-stability additionally means $R(z)\\to0$ as $|z|\\to\\infty$ within that half-plane. The exact method is both A-stable and L-stable, and its amplification factor agrees with backward Euler.\n\n[F1] [[def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map]] gives the real exact-Jacobian equation and update at $\\gamma=1$. Under the real two-dimensional representation in the Statement these are exactly $y^+=y+hk$ and $(1-h\\lambda)k=\\lambda y$; [[thm-rosenbrock-euler-scalar-a-and-l-stability]] uses the same exact complexification and rational-function stability criteria.",

    "minimality": "The exact-J use has a C1/C2 or linear field, so state partial derivatives exist; arbitrary-matrix comparison uses do not need the exact-J branch. Stage equations are unchanged.",
    "route": "separate-maintenance",

    "affected_use": "Let $K\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact and convex, let $f:U\\to\\mathbb R^d$ be continuously differentiable, and suppose $\\|f(y)\\|\\le F$ and $\\|Df(y)\\|\\le L$ on $K$.     "path": "items/thm-rosenbrock-euler-one-newton-step-for-backward-euler.md",
    "affected_use": "Let $f:U\\to\\mathbb R^d$ be continuously differentiable, $y\\in U$, $h>0$, and suppose $M:=I-hDf(y)$ is invertible. The backward-Euler increment equation is $G(s):=s-hf(y+s)=0$. A Newton correction for $G$ at $s_0=0$, defined by $s_1=s_0-DG(s_0)^{-1}G(s_0)$, is $s_1=hM^{-1}f(y)$. Consequently, the state $y+s_1$ equals the exact-arithmetic autonomous exact-Jacobian Rosenbrock-Euler map [[def-rosenbrock-euler-step-map]] with $\\gamma=1$.
